                                              Joseph Perry, Grand Blanc,
                                              Michigan - Co~plainant

                    Mr. Joseph ierrr. -3079 Edwards Street, Grand Blanc, _ .
         Michigan, was contacted at bis request by Agent. or this office
         on .~arch 5, 1960. _                                            . -
                   lir. Perry advised that be operates a Pizza House
         at 3075 Edwards Street, Grand Blanc, and that be bas been a
         professional photographer for thirty years. Be explained
         that be has a hobby of takinZphotograpbs of the .~on through
         a bome-made telescope. At about 1:00 a.m. on February 21,
         1960, be took some photograpbs of the moon and Arter developing
         .L~e, he put them in hie viewer and in one photograph be
         observed what appears to be a flying object -.omewhere between -
         the end or his telescope and the .urface or the moon. Be .aid
         tbat wben blown up, thi. object is flat on tbe bottom, i.
         oval sbaped, and appears to have a fluorescent glow around
         the entire object, and that it i. apparently moving a. it
         bas a vapor trail running behind it.- He bas taken over one
         thousand pictures of the moon and bas never .een anything
         resembling this object.
                   Perry .aid that due to bi. thirty years' experience
         1n photography, be i. aure that what appears on tbe picture
         1s actually a flying object, and 1. not a malfunction of tbe
         camera, is not a blur· on hi. telescope_, and 1. not anything
         that appears on the photo because or the manner in Whicb he
          developed the f1lm. Ferry took about twenty p1c~ea or the
         moon on February 21, 1960, and atter developing them, be
         observed that most or the pictures were under-exposed. 80 be
         Just cut off the- two that-appeared to be properly expoaed and
         destroyed the rest. He furnished both of these pictures to
         the PBI.

                                                                Unidentified J'1YinB Obj ects Interplanetary

         ~,.~:~~~:~ .                                               -
               ':,":',        There is now sufficient information based on data compiled from
                  ... .- eyewitness accounts that a high percentage of aerial phenomena such as
                ':.'l those popularly described as unidentified flying objects are
                 ;r.o'·intelligently controled craft whose ports of origin are' outside
               .'-.',~, ,Earths atmosphe-re."
                  ,~.
                                                 ....      .
             --:~k':~                    .
               ',-",-This is the conclusion reached as the- result of a study begun by
               ':':: Aerial Phenomena Research Group in Seattle, Washington on January 1,
j            '.';:: 19S4 into the field of unidentified flying .objects and possibly
i                        .
                :. .':-rela t ed incidents •
              · :""::- ..


I                    :....                      COJlllllenting on this study, National Director of Aerial Phenome:la
              ~:;/ Research Group Robert J. Gribble saidl "Although the accounts con-
              c.,-;: sidered in this study did not contain scientific mensureDIents of each
                .~\. sighting, it was possible to establish certain valid concluSions by
t             '~:-if\- the application of statistical methods in the'treatment of the data.
                "'>Analyzed and arranged, the data did show a marked pattern.
ti           ", ~~'-~:
              · " ,.-        "On the basis of this marked pattern of the data, A.I' .R.G. has
                  .: concluded that a high percentage of the more than 5,000 accounts of
               · ,i," unidentified flying objects examined in this study represent observ-
                .... "- ations of technologioal developments outside the ;range of present-day
    j         "'~1i scientific knowledge.
    !              - ,"         .                                       I '
    i,       · ?~;                              "Therefore, it is highly probable that many of the unidentified
    r    ---.~;, flying
                   ... -
               ';;':,. p~ane'ts' ...
                                     - ...        objects considered in this study are 'spacecraft from other


                -i;.:":      A.P.R.G. highly recommends that all national security' agencies
                    .r take :immediate steps to strip the unidentified flying objects of' the


I                      special 'secrecy status· they have been given and the aura of mystery
                       :tA.cY.have unfortunatly acquired.    END •

           ~IDENTIFIED FLYING OBJECTS (UFO)                                                                                    ......,- ......,-
                             Stanley Schneider of the Office of Science and
                   ~edhnology, White House, telephonically contacted me today to
                   inquire as to the Bureau's procedure for handling information
                   received from the public or otherwise, relating to sightings
                   of UFOs. He advised that Jody Powell of the President's staff
                   has raised the question 'as to whether or not there is any
                   coordination within the Executive Branch relating to information
                   concerning this subject. He stated that the U.S. Air Force had
                   closed their investigation of these matters several years ago
           '.'
           •
                   and currently does nothing but refer such information to
                   appropriate local authorities.
                             I advised him that as far as the FBI is concerned
                   there appears to be no conceivable jurisdiction for us to oo~Ft
                   any inquiries upon receipt of information relating to a UFO     "'"
                   sighting ~nd, in the absence of some investigative jurisdiction
                   based upon the information furnished, that information would be
                   referred to the Department of the Air Force without any action
                  ~being taken by the Bureau.

           ,                 He thanked me for the information and stated that if
                   any further contact was necessary he would call back.
                   ACTION:                           ~tC·~f%~.~       DE.59      t J - j _ ',;,!_

                                    UFO FACT SHEET

                 On lJecemlJer 17, 1969 ttl\! S",cretarj' of the Air Force
            announced the termination of Project Blue Book, the Air Force
            program for the investigat.ion of UFOS.
                 The decision to discontinup UFO investigations was based
            on an evaluation of a report prepared by the University of
            Colorado entitled, ·Scientific Study of Unidentified Flying
            Objects;- a revie~ of the University of Colorado's report by
            the National Academy of Sciences; past UFO studies; and Air
            Force experience investigating UFO reports during the past
            two decades.
                 As a result of these investigations and studies, and
            experience gained f~om investigating UFO reports since 1~~8,
            the conclusions of Froject Blue Book are:    (1) n" UFO rl.:!-,olted,
            investigated, an-3 evaluated hy tht: Air Force has ever given
            any indication of threat to our rational security; (2) there
            has been no evidence subrnltted ~" or discovered by the Air
            Force that slghtings categorized as -unidentified" represent
            technological developments or principles beyond the range of
            present day scientific knowledge; and (3) there has been no
            evidence indicating that sighting5 categorized as ·unidentified"
            are extraterrestrial vehicles.
                 With the termination of Project Blue Book, the Air Force
            regulation establishing and controlling the program for invest-
            igating and analyzing UFOs was rescinded. All documentation
            regarding the former Blue Book investigation has been per-
            manentl,' transferred to the Modern Hi 1i tary Branch, Uational
            Archives and Records Service, 8th and Pennsylvania Avenue,
            Washington, D.C. 20408, and is available for public review
            and analysis.
                 Attached for your information is the Project Blue Book
            sighting summary for the period 1947-1969. Also included is
            a listing of UFO-related material& currently available.
             Since t~ termination of Project Blue aook, no evidence
        has been pre~nted to indicate that further investigation of
        UFOs by the Air Force is warranted. In view of the consider-
        able Air Force commitment of resources in the past, and the
        extreme pressure on Air Force funds at this time, there is
        no likelihood of renewed Air Force involvement in this area •

Dear Sirs:
                                                                                                          /

     In approximately seven months or.January, 1972, certain copies of top-secret
documents shall be sent to the New York Times as well as two other newspapers.
These documents are related to, and will be an ostentation of thr, involvement of t
pentagon in the controversial i "!:1ni.den.~ifie9._E:lyi!1~ec~s" or' 'F,Jyi}!SL.§e.uce:(."
subject. It will show that not only the U.S. Air Force was involved in UFO resea
but the other military branches as well.

     Analysis and the actual conclusions of the classic UFO cases shall be reveal
This shall be accomplished by zeroxed documents and photographs that General
Wolfe had reviewed when he was head of the Army's UFO support program in the
Pentagon during the Eisenhower years.

     Sorry, but it is concluded here that this is the best course to take because
we feel that the secret UFO investigations are parallel in nature to the Times-
Pentagon Vietnam controversy. If we are wrong in taking this action, time will t,

                                                 TRUE COpy

                     I    May 14, 1969


                          Federal Bureau of Investigation
                          Penn. Ave. & 9th. NW.
                          Wash., D. C. 19806

                          Dear Sirs;

                                          There currently are rumors over the grapevine and
                          in print that suggest men with oriental features wearing dark clothes go
                          around terrorizing people who have had close-up views of UFO's.
                          It is also rumored these creatures have impersonated armed forces officer
                          and FBI investigators. They are supposed to ride around in black or dark
                          automobiles that either have old licence tags or none at all. Several are
                          reported to have attemped to run down witnesses of UFO sightings, made

                        Be:    UNIDENTIFIED FLYING OBJECTS;
                               ANONYMOUS INFORMANT
                               DALLAS, TEXAS
                               OCTOBER 9 , 1967

                              ~
          A young white female, who refused to give her name,
appeared at the Dallas ~Oitice on October 9, 1967. She
stated she is interested in Unidentified Flying Objects (UFOs)
and has received a quantity of information concerning beings
from outer space. She stated she will Dot reveal her identity
as she would feel like a fool if this information is not true.
She stated if it is true, bowever, she will meet with interested
officials and furnish all the intormation she has, provided
nothing is done to endanger her safety.
          She stated in July, 1967, she met a being from another
planet who had assumed earthly form. Be gave ber certain
information. then be was picked up and departed from the earth
on August 21, 1967. She stated she tben received messages from
non-earthly sources in a manner she refused to discuss. She
stated these sources told her of the following:
          (1)    An antimlssile missile was fired at a UFO
                 over Africa on May 22. -1962, but the UFO
                 was protected by its "force field."
          (2)    A UFO was detected 22,000 miles from earth by
                 radar about August 6, 1967.
          (3)    A UFO was detected over Antartica August 20,
                 1967.
          (4)    A UFO was detected over the "Dew line" in the
r,
0'
                 past week and was shot down, and beings from
                 outer space are trying to recover it.
          Informant would furnish no furtber details. She stated
if this information is true, she will know other information

         Tbis document contains neither recommendations
         nor conclusions of the FBI. It is the property
         of the-FBI and is loaned to your agency. It and
         its contents are Dot to be distributed outside
         your agency.
